usage: xpe_calib_fixture_gen --out <dir> [--width N] [--height N]
                             [--seed N] [--expiry-ms N]
                             [--gain-poly] [--poly-degree N] [--poly-levels N]
                             [--caldata <dir>]
                             [--dark F]... [--bright F]... [--dark-list L] [--bright-list L]
                             [--gain-dark-ref mean|none|F] [--compare-bpm F]
                             [--bpm-bright F]... [--bpm-bright-list L]
                             [--bpm-v2] [--bpm-margins L,T,R,B]

Writes offset.xcal, gain.xcal, defect.xcal and manifest.json into <dir>.
The same arguments always produce byte-identical files.

  --gain-poly     also write gain_poly.xcal (XCAL_TYPE_GAIN_POLY), a
                  per-pixel dose-dependent gain polynomial
  --poly-degree N maximum fitted degree, 1-4 (default 2)
  --poly-levels N dose levels to acquire, 3 or more (default 4)
  --caldata <dir> use real frames: dark.raw, bright04..06.raw, BPMap.map
                  (width/height default to 3072; not with --gain-poly)
  --dark/--bright F  one real frame per option (repeat), or --dark-list/--bright-list L
                  (a text file, one path per line). At least 5 dark and 10 bright frames.
  --gain-dark-ref  dark reference for the gain stage: mean (default), none, or a file
  --compare-bpm F  report agreement with someone else's defect map (not used for the set)
  --bpm-bright F   bright frames of the defect map only (several doses allowed); the
                  --bright frames of the gain map must be ONE dose (max/min <= 1.25)
  --bpm-v2        (QA-A-254) defect.xcal from the second-generation chain: pixel map, lines,
                  blobs and the imaging area, written as XCal format version 2 with the line /
                  blob extension (SRS FUNC-039). The first dark frame is skipped (raw list).
  --bpm-margins   L,T,R,B  columns / rows outside the imaging area for --bpm-v2 (default 0,0,0,0)

[perf-gate] SPEC improvement target 60 ms (AVX2, single thread) -- currently 1.8x the target on this machine
[perf-gate-OVER] 3072 ratio=1.720 exceeds limit=1.450 -- ASSERTION SUSPENDED (#179, QA-A-119)
[perf-gate-OVER] 3072x3072 runtime detection: ratio 1.720 exceeds limit 1.450.
  detection  107.1 ms (samples 163.4 / 107.1 / 144.2 / 180.7 / 129.9)
  reference  62.3 ms  (frozen kernel, same process, same warm-up)
  HOW TO READ THIS: the ratio is detection / reference, so a slow
  machine raises BOTH and leaves the ratio alone. A raised ratio means
  the DETECTOR got slower relative to this machine -- that is a code
  regression, and re-running will not clear it.
  If instead the absolutes look high but the ratio is near its usual
  value, the machine is slow and the code is fine.
  (Do NOT read three high samples as proof of a regression: on a slow
  machine all three are high and consistent -- QA-A-59's CI run showed
  exactly that, 1360.9 / 1363.3 / 1340.9 with 1.7% spread.)
